## 12. Expansion model (OUT-SE11-PHYSICAL-INFRASTRUCTURE-HARDWARE-AR-12)

### 12.1 Planned spare capacity at baseline

| Resource | Spare at baseline | Scale path |
|---|---|---|
| Host PCIe | PCI_E1 x16 (reserved for GPU; occupied by the NIC under Fallback A), PCI_E2 x1, 2×USB4 | Scale-up only |
| Host memory | M-A/M-C: two free slots (second matched pair → M-A+, re-validation and an FC-02 row for the new population); M-B/M-A+: none (board maximum) | **Hard ceiling at 256 GB** — REV-MEM |
| Host NVMe slots | None (4/4 populated) | Device-capacity scale-up; SATA ×4 for cold tiers only |
| NAS bays / M.2 | N-A: 4 bays; N-B: 2 bays; 4× M.2 | Same-class drives; cache/metadata |
| NAS network | 1×10 GbE + 2×5 GbE | Second 10 GbE consumer or link redundancy |
| Switch ports | all minus 2–3 at baseline | Workers, gateway, management devices |
| Rack | 18U, 12–17U usable (§230) | Worker trays, second switch, PD-B |
| Power | PD-A margin per §8.2 and the §8.4 runtime reserve; PD-B unused | Workers only on PD-B |
| Worker pool | Elastic: hosted/cloud executors unbounded by local hardware (§168) | Scale-out by default |
| Host agent event log | 1 GB allocation on M2_1 (≥ 7 days / ≥ 100,000 events across incarnations, §18.2) | Allocation raised in the host build; retention never shortened below the contract minimum |

### 12.2 Evidence-driven triggers that add hardware without architecture revision (§159, §171)

| Trigger id | Signal (from §10.3 telemetry) | Action |
|---|---|---|
| TRG-WKR-LOCAL | Build/test queue wait p95 above the SE01-defined threshold for two consecutive stages **and** hosted-runner cost or data privacy makes local execution preferable (§168, §171) **and** the limiting factor is worker capacity, not central resources, storage, network, provider quotas or software (§171) | Adopt local worker class (B19), rack, PDU, UPS-B (B16/B17) |
| TRG-GPU | Measured external-model latency/cost/privacy case or GPU-required application testing (§176) with §5.5 preconditions met (including release of PCI_E1 from Fallback A) | Adopt B11 |
| TRG-MEM-EXPAND (M-A/M-C only) | Measured committed memory p95 > 80 % of installed for one stage **and** the §5.3 procedure records Steps 1 and 2 as failed | Add the second matched pair (M-A+; FC-02 row; re-run SE11-AC-04 memory pass); record SE11-DEC |
| TRG-NAS-MEM | NAS memory sustained > 70 % or snapshot/backup jobs missing their window from memory pressure | Install B13 |
| TRG-NAS-CAP | T2 utilisation > 70 % | Add same-class drives to free bays (N-A → N-B) |
| TRG-NET-10G | L1 utilisation > 70 % sustained during backup windows, or a second 10 GbE consumer | Use NAS second 10 GbE / add 10 GbE ports within the switch class |
| TRG-NVME-TBW | Any NVMe > 80 % of rated TBW or media errors (M2_2 watched closely when scratch is on it) | Replace with same class (§11.4) |
| TRG-UPS-BATT | Any UPS runtime estimate at measured load < B_u / 0.7 | Battery replacement (same class) or §8.4 remedies |
| TRG-LREPO | A product or governance decision requires a local repository server (e.g. §66 confidential source) | Adopt `LREPO` class as a D-IRR class through §6.3 (fixture, ARMED, admission, store registration with SE11-AC-07e) |

### 12.3 Scale-out versus scale-up boundaries and architecture-revision triggers

The central node complements, never replaces, horizontally scalable workers (§177). Scale-out (more workers, hosted executors) is the default answer to throughput; scale-up applies only to the capabilities the seed assigns to the central node — large contiguous RAM, low-latency CPU, NVMe, centralized data/control services (§161). The following require a revision of this architecture (new SE11 version plus EL review), not a hardware addition:

| Revision trigger | Why adding hardware is insufficient |
|---|---|
| REV-MEM: §5.3 Step 4 (1.25 F > 256 GB with hosted placement inadmissible), or Highest-priority pool floors exceed 85 % of 256 GB on measured p95 | Board ceiling reached; second host, larger-memory platform class, or data-tier redesign is an architectural choice |
| REV-HA: an SLO (§117) requires the control plane or Data VM to survive FD-HOST or FD-NVME-M2_4, or requires unattended restart that no admissible combination provides | Needs a second failure domain and quorum design (§173) |
| REV-GPU-CLASS: accelerator > 145 W, dual-slot, or second accelerator | Exceeds PSU, slot and thermal envelope (§5.5) |
| REV-STORAGE-HOT: T1 > 85 % after device scale-up; a random-IO workload that cannot stay on host NVMe; §6.1a remedy R2 (Validation's demand cannot fit beside Build and Mapping-V is unavailable or insufficient) | NAS is not a hot tier (§164); host NVMe topology exhausted |
| REV-STORAGE: retention volume V > 44 TiB (§6.7) | Beyond the enclosure's economical RAID 6 envelope; hosted tier or second enclosure is architectural |
| REV-NET: storage/core path saturated after all 10 GbE ports used, or 25 GbE class needed | Switch and NIC class change ripples through host slots |
| REV-SITE: geographic/resilience footprint beyond one room (§226 F.20) | Hosted/hybrid footprint decision |
| REV-POWER: §8.4 remedies exhausted, PD-A measured peak within 20 % of UPS-A rating, circuit limits reached, or no admissible restart combination meets the SLO | UPS/circuit redesign by qualified professionals; possible relocation |


## 13. Security considerations (OUT-SE11-PHYSICAL-INFRASTRUCTURE-HARDWARE-AR-11)

The owners of the control catalogue (SE17 for infrastructure/workload/data security, SE15 for identity and cryptography, §74) have not published control ids in this run (their documents are absent, §0.1). This section therefore does two separable things: **(a)** it states the hardware security requirements under SE11's identifiers and, for each, the check that **SE11 itself executes and can pass without any owner catalogue** (SE11-REQ-19, SE11-AC-08a) — implementable from this document alone; **(b)** it names what remains the owner's (policy, control ids, long-term custody, identity) as placed items, unmet dependency SE11-DEP-05, verified later as SE11-AC-08b. No foreign control id is invented; nothing re-derives a control design. The binding of each SE11-SECREQ to an owner control id is the owner's act (§18.3 row CI-4); until then SE11-SECREQ-nn are the ids of record for the hardware baseline.

| Id | Requirement placed on the hardware baseline | (a) SE11-executable check (SE11-AC-08a) — pass criterion | (b) Owner part (SE11-AC-08b, placed; SE11-DEP-05) |
|---|---|---|---|
| SE11-SECREQ-01 Trusted boot | UEFI Secure Boot enabled; TPM 2.0 present and enabled; boot order locked to M2_1; UEFI administrator password set; the RTC-wake and AC-recovery settings of §8.3b are part of the protected firmware baseline | Firmware settings export shows Secure Boot = on, TPM 2.0 = enabled and owned, boot order = M2_1 only, admin password = set, AC-recovery = power on, RTC wake = enabled; host OS reports Secure Boot state on; export checksum recorded in the fingerprint | SE17: measured-boot attestation decision and control id |
| SE11-SECREQ-02 Firmware integrity | Vendor-signed images only, checksum verified, staged in maintenance windows with snapshot + verified backup first (§10.4); firmware settings baseline re-verified after every BIOS update | Each firmware record in the inventory carries the vendor checksum and the verification result; post-update settings export matches the baseline | SE17 (policy, control id), SE20 (execution) |
| SE11-SECREQ-03 Management-plane isolation and bootstrap authority confinement | All management endpoints (host admin, hypervisor, NAS admin, switch, UPS software, host agent metrics/events/evidence endpoint, shutdown/WOL command paths, the S-2 UPS-service client path) only on `seg.mgmt`, unreachable from `seg.build`, `seg.worker`, `seg.egress`; no management interface exposed to the internet; the NAS shutdown command and the evidence intake are authenticated; **during the bootstrap window Part B/C have no network listener and the local channel admits only `svc-se11-agent`; after the window the local channel does not exist** (§6.3c, I7) | Port-scan from a host in each of `seg.build`, `seg.worker`, `seg.egress` reaches no management endpoint; from the gateway side no management port is reachable; the NAS shutdown command and an evidence POST fail without credentials (SE11-AC-05 segmentation log); SE11-AC-07d step 9 (d)–(g) pass | SE17: inter-segment policy and control id; SE15: MFA, least-privilege, short-lived credentials per §59/§112 |
| SE11-SECREQ-04 Physical and administrative access | Lockable room; host, NAS, switch and UPS inside; the T3-SUB media outside it under the KB-procedure; physical console use is a logged administrative action; segregation between hardware maintenance (SE11/SE20) and security configuration (SE17) per §112 | Room lock verified; devices inside recorded by inventory position; console sessions appear in the host audit log; T3-SUB custody log present | SE17: custody rule for T3-SUB after takeover, access-control id, segregation control |
| SE11-SECREQ-05 Data at rest | Host full-disk encryption on M2_1 with TPM-bound keys (recovery key under KB-3a before enabling); encryption of VM disks on M2_2/M2_3/M2_4 inside the Linux VMs or by the hypervisor (hardware supports both); NAS per-folder AES-256 (B12) for backups, evidence, mirrors and archives; T3 and T3-SUB copies encrypted before leaving the site; the `KEYS` class under §6.3 | Host volume reports encryption on with TPM protector and the recovery key read back from both T3-SUB media; each VM disk or guest volume reports encryption on; NAS shares for T2 report encryption on; a T3/T3-SUB object sampled at rest is ciphertext | SE17/SE15: long-term key custody, recovery and takeover (KB-5); control ids |
| SE11-SECREQ-06 Media sanitisation | NVMe, HDD and T3-SUB media sanitised on decommissioning/RMA; inventory record marks sanitised state | Inventory field present and required for state "decommissioned" | SE17: sanitisation standard and control id |
| SE11-SECREQ-07 Supply chain at receipt (G2) | Serial numbers recorded; packaging/tamper evidence inspected; firmware version read and compared with the G1 row before first power-on; default credentials on NAS, switch and UPS software rotated at commissioning with the new credentials entering `KEYS` under KB-3d | Receipt record per asset with serial, inspection result, firmware version and comparison; credential rotation logged with the KEYS evidence ids | SE17: supply-chain control id |
| SE11-SECREQ-08 Confidential-processing boundary | Private application/customer data is processed only by authorised locally hosted models or deterministic tools (§66); any local accelerator (B11) and the Data VM sit inside `seg.platform` behind the default-deny disclosure gateway on `seg.egress`; hardware telemetry, leases and evidence records (§10.3, §18.2) carry no workload content; `REPO-EXT` holds no customer data; candidate M-C (hosted data) is admissible only where §66 permits | Telemetry and evidence payloads sampled during SE11-AC-09 contain only the §18.2 fields; `seg.platform` has no route to `seg.egress` except via the gateway address (SE11-AC-05) | SE17/EL: gateway policy, control id; §66 applicability decision for M-C and for `REPO-EXT` contents |


## 14. Validation / evidence plan (OUT-SE11-PHYSICAL-INFRASTRUCTURE-HARDWARE-AR-14)

Gates are ordered G1 (documents only) → G2 (receipt) → G3 (commissioning); a later gate never closes an earlier one (§11.2). Executing owners are named; where the owner's test or operations contract does not yet exist the dependency id is given and SE11's own pass criterion is separated from the owner's. Every acceptance record is committed to `REPO-EXT` (§6.2), outside the local admission scope, so no evidence depends on a local protection class. Protection tests use the authoritative transition table of §6.3a and the lease rule of §6.3b; a **test class `TEST`** with A_max = G = 600 s and U unchanged (300 s) is registered for drills whose duration would otherwise be 48 h, with one unscaled confirmation per stage on a real class in the expiry drill.

| AC id | Gate | Check | Pass criterion | Evidence artefact | Executing owner | Needed before |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

**(b) consumer restart with a saved cursor** across a publisher restart → `409 incarnation_changed` + snapshot; the consumer adopts the snapshot's levels, leases and last edges and the new `(inc, latest)`; STALE asserted only for the publisher's actual downtime if > 180 s; **(c) expired history** within one incarnation → `410 history_expired` + snapshot; the consumer adopts it (a toggled level is reflected) and no transition is silently lost; **(d) store loss** → host agent store wiped; publisher restarts with a new `inc` and `seq` = 1; the consumer's old cursor yields `incarnation_changed`, never events, never `cursor_ahead`; **(e) cursor ahead** within the same incarnation (corrupted consumer store) → `409 cursor_ahead` + snapshot, consumer adopts; **(f) bootstrap** → a fresh consumer with the endpoint unreachable reports every class as not bootstrapped and `admit` = DENY until a snapshot or complete INIT set from the current incarnation is received (SE11-REQ-22); **(g) same-second double restart (new)** → the publisher is restarted twice within one second with its store wiped between restarts while the consumer holds a cursor from the first incarnation and the publisher had issued > 10 events per second before the restarts; the consumer receives `incarnation_changed` on its next poll; every `(inc, seq)` pair observed across the three incarnations is unique; no SET/CLEAR is skipped (the snapshot's levels equal the publisher's persisted state); **(h) clock earlier than the last issued event (new)** → the host clock is set 1 h earlier than the `ts` of the last issued event and the publisher restarted → new `inc`, `seq` from 1, `ts` regression logged as a clock anomaly; | 5 | DB: SE12's store registers as producer **with its SE11-AC-07e record** and a Part C token issued through the KB-3d path; SE11-AC-07b-init(DB) with a synthetic database through its backup path to T3 (provider per NT16, or T3-SUB) and restore → `MECH_FIXTURE_OK` → **ARMED**. *If SE12's store does not exist or has no AC-07e record:* the validator refuses the registration, DB stays MECH-UNVERIFIED, no real DB data can exist, and the trace ends legally at step 8 with KEYS as the only real class | SE12 store token | COPY | ARMED | MECH-UNVERIFIED | KEYS only | PL-T3 | I6 ✓ |
| 6 | SE12's store admits the first real database on a bootstrapped, non-STALE `admit(DB)` = ALLOW → `DATASET_CREATED` → **ANCHOR** (lease `until` = t_create + 48 h); initial copy within A_max → `COPY_OK` + `RESTORE_OK` → **COPY** | SE12 store token | COPY | ANCHOR → COPY | MECH-UNVERIFIED | KEYS, DB | PL-T3-PENDING → PL-T3 | I1 ✓, I5 ✓ (ANCHOR ≤ A_max), I3 (only if the copy misses A_max → DEGRADED, bounded; mutation bounded by the lease) |
| 7 | EVID: SE12's evidence storage registers (AC-07e record, token); fixture package → ARMED; first real release-evidence package (produced by EL/SP14) admitted → ANCHOR → COPY | SE12 store token | COPY | COPY | ANCHOR → COPY | KEYS, DB, EVID | PL-T3-PENDING → PL-T3 | I1 ✓, I5 ✓ |
| 8 | `REPO-EXT` T2 mirror established and refreshed ≤ 1 h; repository bundle written to T3-SUB (D-REB; no class state changes) | — | COPY | COPY | COPY | unchanged | PL-T3 | — |
| 9 | **Negative checks:** (a) a store attempts to create a real dataset of a class in MECH-UNVERIFIED → `admit` = DENY → refused; a `DATASET_CREATED` nevertheless submitted → 409 and enforcement-violation suspension (observed with the bootstrap store against a de-registered test class); (b) an evidence record from an unregistered producer id, or from a registered producer for a class it does not own, is rejected (403); (c) the validator rejects a store registration that names no class or carries no AC-07e record; **(d)** a call on the local channel after step 4b fails (socket absent); **(e)** during the window (re-executed on a scratch host build), a process under an OS identity other than `svc-se11-agent` calling the local channel is refused by the ACL and logged; **(f)** during the window, a network call to the Part B/C ports is refused (no listener); **(g)** a host build setting `bootstrap_window = true` while token ids exist in the inventory is refused by the validator; (h) a Part B/C call with an unknown or revoked token → 401 | mixed | — | — | — | unchanged | PL-T3 | I1 ✓, I6 ✓, I7 ✓ |
| 10 | **Recurring:** at the next stage gate SE11-AC-07b-rec runs for every class with real data using fresh fixtures; a pass refreshes `mech_ok` only; a fail sets SUSPECT and `admit` DENY and lets the class age per §6.3a — the gate is never made impossible by the presence of real data | store tokens | COPY | COPY | COPY | unchanged | PL-T3 | I3 ✓ (bounded), P1b ✓ |

Resilience tests beyond the above (fault injection on `pool.validate`, switch loss, NAS loss, provider loss, uplink loss, external-repository loss with mirror promotion, publisher loss across a lease deadline on a real class) are specified and run by SE19 against the §9 domains; SE11 supplies the physical means (spare cables, drive-pull and power-cut procedures) and accepts the evidence.


## 15. Exclusions (BND-SE11-PHYSICAL-INFRASTRUCTURE-HARDWARE-AR-01 … AR-04)

| Exclusion | Disposition in this document |
|---|---|
| BND-01 Purchase orders, price negotiation, vendor-selection scorecards, procurement workflow | Excluded. No prices, suppliers, order quantities or purchase approvals appear; part numbers are cited only as the seed's reference exemplars (SEL-0) and are not baselined (SE11-DEC-14); alternatives are by specification class (§11.4); G1 is a technical feasibility gate, not a procurement step. Nobody owns procurement in this chain. The external repository and T3 providers are referenced by role; their selection is NT16's (SE11-DEP-09). |
| BND-02 Project-owned acquisition dates | Excluded. Only dependency windows are referenced ("Stage 1", "before the first real dataset of the class", "before any purchase or build"); no calendar dates. |
| BND-03 Detailed software/container/service design owned by existing SE11 artefacts | Excluded. §4–§6 stop at pools, VM boundaries, hypervisor reservation controls, limiter configuration, cgroup budget boundaries and volume mapping; service design, images, pipelines and the scheduler's shedding controller are referenced to EL and SE11's runtime artefacts. The host agent is specified only by its contracts (§18.2), its state machine (§8.3), its transition and lease rules (§6.3a/b), its bootstrap authority (§6.3c) and its storage (§6.1), not as a service design. |
| BND-04 Unverified assumptions presented as confirmed hardware facts | Complied with. Every non-seed figure is an engineering assumption (ASM-SE11-nn) or a validation gate (SE11-AC-nn); support, compatibility and availability are explicitly **not established** (§11.2, FC-01 … FC-09c) and part numbers are deferred; SEL-0 is labelled a candidate list; no vendor protocol, restart or compatibility capability is asserted (§8.3); no claim that the class baseline is feasible is made; the two unmet gates are declared (§0.2). |


## 16. Architecture risks, decisions and ADRs (OUT-SE11-PHYSICAL-INFRASTRUCTURE-HARDWARE-AR-13)

### 16.1 Architecture decision records (all PROV, §0.2)

| ADR | Decision | Alternatives considered | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| SE11-ADR-01 | The **candidate** physical architecture is one central node + durable NAS + elastic hosted-first worker pool. Each local element passes gate **G-LOCAL** before it is provisioned: workload evidence *and* economics justify it (§238), and no managed/hosted equivalent better satisfies the target requirements. Until G-LOCAL evidence exists (SE11-DEP-03/08), every pool may be delivered locally or hosted | (a) Two-node local cluster from day one — rejected now: no measured need, violates no-overbuild (§158). (b) All-hosted — **not rejected**: the seed's acceptable equivalent (§238, §160) and the fallback for FD-HOST/FD-ROOM; §66 favours local handling of confidential data but does not mandate local hardware | §160, §226, §238 |
| SE11-ADR-02 | Windows 11 Pro host with Hyper-V Linux VMs for all durable services; WSL2 for interactive use only | Bare-metal Linux hypervisor — rejected: the seed fixes the preferred operating model (§166) and retains Windows for workstation/admin use (§227.9); portability is preserved at the VM/container-definition level (§168) | §166–§168 |
| SE11-ADR-03 | Non-ECC DDR5 on the consumer AM5 platform accepted for the central node, monitored | ECC workstation/server class — recorded substitute if memory-error telemetry or data-integrity incidents attributable to memory occur (SE11-RISK-02) | §227.4; durable tier (NAS) is ECC (§228.1) |
| SE11-ADR-04 | GPU deferred; adoption gated by §5.5 and TRG-GPU; accelerator class capped at single-slot ≤ 145 W within this architecture | Adopt at first build — rejected: §175/§177 make it optional and demand-driven; external providers are the default (§160) | §175–§177 |
| SE11-ADR-05 | Host NVMe as independent volumes without RAID; NAS as durable/backup tier only; one-way replication T1 → T2 → T3 | Host NVMe mirroring — rejected (does not address FD-HOST; doubles cost); NAS as live VM datastore — rejected (§164: not a latency substitute; would make FD-STORAGE a hot-path SPOF) | §163, §164 |
| SE11-ADR-06 | UPS signalling is a **gated** selection among S-1 (preferred), S-2 and S-3 (§8.3), decided by SE11-DEC-10 on G1 evidence; shutdown order NAS-command ‖ Build ‖ Validation → Core → Data → host; per-candidate budgets; restart by an admissible combination (§8.3a) with per-topology eligibility (§8.3b-S5) | v1 chose S-2 without compatibility evidence — demoted to a candidate; firmware AC-recovery alone as the restart mechanism — rejected (does not fire without an AC-input cycle) | §165, §228.1, §229.1 |
| SE11-ADR-07 | Single managed switch, ≥ 2×10 GbE minimum (seed), ≥ 4×10 GbE preferred, adopted ahead of the rack (conflict C4), powered from PD-A (conflict C1); interim point-to-point 10 GbE allowed as placement | Unmanaged switch — rejected (segmentation needs VLANs); dual redundant switches — rejected now (§166 "purchased incrementally from measured need"); no switch until the rack — rejected (management segment, VLAN enforcement, N1 forwarding and the S-2 client path need it) | §165, §166, §230 |
| SE11-ADR-08 | Capacity **quantities** (memory kit, NAS drive count) are candidates governed by decision rules (§5.3, §6.7) and the seed's H.1/H.3 default to the smaller configuration; the maximum listed configuration is never adopted on the strength of this document or of a single oversized component | Adopt the seed's listed quantities as given — rejected: ENV-DEC-017, §162, §238; single-component trigger to the maximum — rejected | §158, §162, §234, §238 |
| SE11-ADR-09 | Storage mapping places Highest-priority VM disks on M2_4 and High/Medium VM disks + artifacts on M2_2 (§6.1), deviating from the BOM's literal role labels (C2), with a mandatory, device-bounded limiter for Validation versus Build on M2_2 in every lane × QoS combination (§6.1a) | Literal seed mapping (all VMs on M2_2) with IOPS controls only — rejected: puts Highest and Medium pools on one device, contrary to §157 and SE11-REQ-02; device separation alone as the QoS fallback — rejected: leaves Validation unprotected; a fixed 60 % Build cap with an unbounded Validation minimum — rejected (can exceed the device) | §157, §163, §174 |
| SE11-ADR-10 | At the hardware layer, priority is implemented as **weighted contention** (hypervisor reserves/limits/weights, in-VM cgroup weights) with measurable guarantees (SE11-NFR-01); **load shedding** is a scheduler action owned by EL/SE01 and driven by SE11-published signals (SE11-DEC-11); SE11 designs no shedding controller and no SE11 guarantee depends on it | Hardware-layer "throttling order" claim — rejected: not implementable without a controller; an SE11-owned shedding controller — rejected: §175 assigns it to the scheduler, and it is service design (BND-03) | §157, §174, §175 |
| SE11-ADR-11 | Where M1 is not evidenced and the host has a direct UPS link, restart uses a **wake-poll loop** (M3: RTC alarm re-armed on every on-battery wake until mains is present or the UPS exhausts) with a derived timing budget; single-alarm M3 and attended M4 are inadmissible for the automatic-restart requirement | Single RTC alarm — rejected (an unsuccessful wake left the platform halted until exhaustion or an operator); an attempt cap on the loop — rejected (reopens the same gap) | §165, §229.1 |
| SE11-ADR-12 | When Validation's measured I/O demand cannot fit beside Build on M2_2 under the §6.1a identity, the first remedy is **Mapping-V** (Validation isolated on the M2_3 device, scratch moved to M2_2 inside Build's budget); only if Mapping-V is unavailable (Fallback B) or insufficient is the outcome an architecture revision (REV-STORAGE-HOT) | Shrinking Build's floor below its useful minimum — rejected; time-slicing Validation and Build via the scheduler — rejected (scheduler policy, not a hardware guarantee); adding a fifth NVMe — not available (4/4 M.2 slots populated) | §157, §163, §174 |
| SE11-ADR-13 (revised) | Restart combinations are admitted only by the **matrix of §8.3a derived from rule R-WAKE** (a device is woken only by an AC cycle or by a command from a device that has itself confirmed mains online *for the woken device's power domain* through a direct UPS link), and every admitted combination has a **defined S5 status source and fail-safe for each device** (§8.3b-S5). Consequences: S-2 admits M1 only, with the host's S5 status read through the NAS UPS service, a bounded wait and HOST-ONLY-HOLD as the fail-safe; S-3 NAS eligibility is established by UPS-N independently (M1-N) or, for N1, only when UPS-N shares the host's circuit and the NAS guards itself after the wake | Letting the host wake the NAS first under S-2 and then query it — rejected (boots a device on an unknown power state); S-2/M3 with a "poll until the NAS answers" loop — rejected (never terminates with the NAS off); admitting S-2/M1 with the host "assuming mains" after the output cycle — rejected (the host could start VMs on a second outage it cannot see); S-3/N1 on any circuit — rejected (the host would wake the NAS on an unknown power domain) | §165, §229.1, §231 |
| SE11-ADR-14 | **Bootstrap boundary:** the authoritative repository (`REPO-EXT`) is external and outside the local admission scope; local clones and the T2 mirror are D-REB; commissioning evidence is committed there; `KEYS` is bootstrapped by an SE11-owned custody procedure on T3-SUB media so that the first real local D-IRR class can be admitted without any absent owner and without an exemption; a local repository server is a later class (`LREPO`) | Treating the local repository as the first D-IRR class protected "from day one" — rejected (circular); exempting commissioning evidence from the invariants — rejected | §160, §164, §31 |
| SE11-ADR-15 (revised) | Event protocol with an **incarnation-aware cursor `(inc, seq)`**: `inc` is a 128-bit random value generated at every publisher start, `seq` is gap-free from 1 within an incarnation; the API accepts and compares `inc` and answers a mismatch with an explicit `409 incarnation_changed` + snapshot handshake; snapshots carry levels, leases and the last edge events; wall-clock time never enters identity; a 60 s heartbeat snapshot with a 180 s stale bound; bounded retention; INIT on start and a consumer bootstrap condition | A wall-clock floor `unix_seconds × 10^6` after counter loss — rejected (same-second restart or clock rollback reuses or decreases identifiers); per-epoch `seq` with `epoch` as a diagnostic only — rejected (a consumer could never observe the change); a message broker — rejected (BND-03; a broker inside a VM is unavailable exactly when the host agent must publish) | §30, §160 |
| SE11-ADR-16 (new) | **Mutation lease:** mutation authorization for real local D-IRR data is a self-expiring lease derived from verified copy timestamps (`until` = max(`t3_last_ok` + A_max + G, `t3sub_last_ok` + A_max)) and enforced by each store against its own clock with margin U, independently of publisher reachability; STALE/UNKNOWN neither extends nor is needed to end it; a store proves enforcement (SE11-AC-07e) before it may register | FREEZE immediately on STALE/UNKNOWN — rejected (makes every store's availability depend on the publisher's; a host-agent restart would freeze the platform); publisher-only `mutate` level — rejected (unbounded under publisher loss, the reviewer's finding); a lease renewed by heartbeats — rejected (a renewal is not evidence of a copy) | §164, §31, §112 |
| SE11-ADR-17 (new) | **Bootstrap authority:** during a bounded bootstrap window Part B/C are served only on an OS-authenticated local channel to the SE11 bootstrap store, with no network listener; the window closes by a committed flag flip and host-agent restart that enables token-authenticated network endpoints and removes the channel; re-opening requires a rebuilt host with an empty inventory | A pre-shared bootstrap token baked into the host build — rejected (a secret that exists before KEYS custody, violating I7 and §112); a token-less network endpoint during the window — rejected (reachable from the LAN); permanent local channel alongside tokens — rejected (an unrevoked authority) | §112, §59, §164 |

UPS-service autostart, and drill (k)), S-3 automatic fallback (topology substitution of B15, C3); none relied on before G1 and SE11-AC-06/14/15 | §8.3; SE11-RISK-11 |

| SE11-DEC-13 | Restart after shutdown: M1 (commanded UPS output power-cycle with auto-restart) is mandatory where evidenced for the device that owns the UPS link; M3 (wake-poll loop) only for a host with a direct UPS link; N1/M1-N for the NAS per §8.3a; M4 is never a pass for SE11-REQ-15 | §8.3a/b, SE11-REQ-15, SE11-RISK-12 |
| SE11-DEC-14 | Every seed part number (B1–B17) is DEFERRED-PENDING-EVIDENCE and a member of SEL-0; the baseline is bound to specification classes (§11.4) and candidate ids; a part number enters the configuration fingerprint only with a "confirmed" G1 row; the class baseline does not itself establish feasibility (FC-01 … FC-09c open) | §1.2, §11.2, §11.3, §17 |
| SE11-DEC-15 | Memory admission by **Req = max(1.25 F, C + 8 GB) ≤ Installed**; floors are fixed inputs; Step 1 may only raise floors to demand or lower maxima to minima; Step 2 hosted placement; Step 3 next candidate; Step 4 REV-MEM; every step recorded in SE11-DEC-03a | §5.2, §5.3, SE11-REQ-14 |

| SE11-DEC-17 | M1 output-off delay D_off = (worst-case last-device-off − command time) + 60 s per candidate; M3 as a re-arming wake-poll loop with W = 300 s; T_restart ≤ 720 s to S6 entry; T_service_clean ≤ 1,200 s and T_service_forced ≤ 1,800 s to service readiness (§8.3b) | §8.3b, SE11-REQ-20, SE11-NFR-03 |

| SE11-DEC-19 | **Admissibility matrix** of signalling candidate × restart mechanism (§8.3a) derived from rule R-WAKE; S-2 admits M1 only; S-2/M3 inadmissible; the configuration validator refuses any inadmissible combination | §8.3a, SE11-AC-03, SE11-AC-15 (g) |
| SE11-DEC-20 | Protection inputs enter SE11's publisher only through the SE11-owned producer contract `SE11-CONTRACT-protection-evidence@1`; producers (stores) register per class; absence of evidence is the fail-safe; a class without a registered producer is MECH-UNVERIFIED (I6) | §6.3, §18.2 Part C, SE11-REQ-18 |
| SE11-DEC-21 | Ordered gates G1 (documentary feasibility and selection, documents only) → G2 (receipt) → G3 (commissioning); a later gate never closes an earlier one (FC-02 corrected accordingly); SEL-0 recorded as the candidate reference set; QC-AR-03 explicitly UNMET until G1 holds a confirmed configuration | §11.2, §11.3, §14 |
| SE11-DEC-22 (revised) | Event protocol parameters: identity `(inc, seq)` with `inc` random per publisher start and `seq` gap-free from 1; API compares `inc`; `409 incarnation_changed`/`409 cursor_ahead`/`410 history_expired` each with a snapshot (levels, leases, last edges); heartbeat H = 60 s, consumer poll P ≤ 60 s, stale bound 3H = 180 s on *received* heartbeats; retention ≥ 7 days and ≥ 100,000 events; `levels` snapshot; INIT on start with `UNKNOWN` as fail-safe; consumer bootstrap condition before admission | §18.2 Part B, SE11-REQ-22, SE11-AC-09 |
| SE11-DEC-23 (new) | **Mutation lease** per §6.3b: publisher derives `until`; every registered store enforces now < `until − U` by its own clock with U = 300 s, persists the lease, rejects skewed leases, freezes on clock rollback, resumes only on a fresh lease; registration requires an SE11-AC-07e record | §6.3, §6.3b, SE11-REQ-24, §18.2, SE11-AC-07e |
| SE11-DEC-24 (new) | **Bootstrap authority** per §6.3c: local OS-authenticated channel during the bootstrap window, no network listener, static registration `auth = local-channel`, closure by flag flip + restart, channel removed, validator rules | §6.3c, SE11-REQ-25, SE11-AC-07d |
| SE11-DEC-25 (new) | **Per-topology restart eligibility** (§8.3b-S5): S-2/M1 host status via the NAS UPS service with W_s2 and HOST-ONLY-HOLD; S-3 NAS eligibility by UPS-N independently (M1-N) or N1 only with `ups_n_circuit = A` and FC-09c; timing recomputed; drills (k)–(m) | §8.3a, §8.3b, SE11-REQ-26, SE11-AC-15 |
| SE11-DEC-26 (new) | **Authoritative transition table** (§6.3a) with precedence P1–P4; fixture guard; substitute-copy rule (verified on a second machine before it authorizes anything); `rfail` with EXPIRED at 2; duplicate/out-of-order handling; §14 and §18.2 Part C reference it | §6.3a, SE11-AC-07f |

### 16.3 Requirements introduced by this document (SE11-REQ-nn; all PROV)

| Id | Requirement | Verified by |
|---|---|---|
| SE11-REQ-01 | Hard reservations (static memory, 100 % processor reserve) for `pool.core`, `pool.data`, `pool.runtime`; every other pool has a declared floor/ceiling or an explicit shared-budget membership with a stated share | SE11-AC-03, SE11-AC-12 |
| SE11-REQ-02 | No Highest-priority pool shares a physical NVMe device with a Medium-priority pool | SE11-AC-02/03 (mapping §6.1) |
| SE11-REQ-03 | T0/T1 is never the sole copy of D-IRR or D-BND data beyond the declared windows: every T1 dataset has a T2 copy within the snapshot interval; every real local D-IRR class is at every moment in exactly one §6.3 state, its exposure to total local loss is bounded by that state (zero beyond `age(c)` with COPY; the class for at most A_max + G with ANCHOR) and its mutation exposure is bounded by the lease (§6.3b) | SE11-AC-07a–f |
| SE11-REQ-04 | L1 sustains ≥ 0.7 GB/s host↔NAS under concurrent build-cache load with the NIC in its actual slot; shared paths are not the first bottleneck | SE11-AC-05 |
| SE11-REQ-05 | VM/container definitions carry no physical slot ids, serials or host names (portability §160) | SE11-AC-03 review |
| SE11-REQ-06 | For every UPS of the selected candidate, B_u ≤ 0.7 × measured R_u; every step bounded; fail-closed if unmet | SE11-AC-06, SE11-AC-14 |
| SE11-REQ-07 | Inventory identity scheme, record fields and configuration fingerprint (§10.1, §11.3) for every asset and cable; records committed to `REPO-EXT` | SE11-AC-10 |
| SE11-REQ-08 | Telemetry signals of §10.3, including the per-class protection record, leases and the SE11-DEC-11 events, exported from first power-on under `SE11-CONTRACT-hardware-signals@1` | SE11-AC-09 |
| SE11-REQ-09 | Firmware/driver lifecycle per §10.4 with snapshot + verified backup precondition and G1 row re-opening | SE11-AC-08a, SE20 |
| SE11-REQ-10 | Electrical circuits verified and, where needed, installed by a licensed electrician against §8.2 load figures, with the sign-off identifying the branch circuit of every UPS receptacle; SE11 designs no electrical work | SE11-AC-06 sign-off reference |
| SE11-REQ-11 | Downstream documents cite this document id **with its status (PROVISIONAL, BLOCKED in v6)**, the B-id + class disposition + quantity status they rely on, the §6.1a row/mapping, the §8.3a combination with its S5 status source, and the §9 failure-domain ids | EL review (SE11-DEP-02) |
| SE11-REQ-12 | Any substitution of a dispositioned item is recorded as a new SE11-DEC with technical reason and impact before use; silent substitution is prohibited | SE11-AC-10 |
| SE11-REQ-13 | Protection activation: a real local D-IRR dataset of class c may be created only while `admit(c)` = ALLOW on a bootstrapped, non-STALE level (c ARMED for the first dataset or PROTECTED with `rfail` = 0 and mech_state OK for further ones); never in MECH-UNVERIFIED, DEGRADED or EXPIRED; in `PL-0`/`PL-T2` only D-BND/D-REB and synthetic data exist locally | SE11-AC-07b/d/f, SE11-AC-10 |
| SE11-REQ-14 | Reservation mechanism: hypervisor processor reserve/limit/weight, static vs dynamic memory per Table 5.3-R admitted by Req ≤ Installed (§5.2), storage limiter per §6.1a, in-VM cgroup shares | SE11-AC-03, SE11-AC-12 |
| SE11-REQ-15 | An admissible combination of §8.3a, with a defined S5 status source and fail-safe for each device (§8.3b-S5), that re-powers host **and** NAS after a controlled shutdown without depending on an AC-input cycle is commissioned; attended-only paths never satisfy this requirement; the low-charge rules of S5 apply | SE11-AC-15, SE11-AC-06 (c)(d)(e) |
| SE11-REQ-16 | Validation versus Build storage protection: for the row/mapping in force, a limiter (hypervisor QoS or guest `io.max` on `build.slice`) enforces V_min / B_max under the §6.1a identity with the fixed per-device shares; the feasibility test is evaluated at every calibration; Fallback B combinations are admissible only on a passed SE11-AC-12b | SE11-AC-03, SE11-AC-12b |
| SE11-REQ-17 (revised) | Per-class protection record (§6.3 field table), states and `admit` derived **only** by the §6.3a transition table and precedence; lease derived only by §6.3b; fixture records never advance real-data timestamps; a substitute copy authorizes mutation only after its matched real-copy `RESTORE_OK` on a second machine; evidence ingested only under Part C | SE11-AC-07b/c/d/f, SE11-AC-09, SE11-AC-10 |
| SE11-REQ-18 (revised) | The store that holds each local D-IRR class registers as a Part C producer **with a passed SE11-AC-07e record for its build**, enforces `admit(c)` from the published levels and the lease rule of §6.3b in its own code: SE11's bootstrap store for `KEYS` during commissioning; SE12's stores for `DB`/`EVID`; SE17/SE15's store for `KEYS` after takeover; EL's Definition of Ready names the class, store and AC-07e record for every new data-bearing service; SE20's checklist is the process gate. A class with no such registered producer holds no real data (I6) | SE11-AC-07c/d/e/f, validator (SE11-REQ-23) |
| SE11-REQ-19 | The hardware security checks of §13 column (a) are executed and passed by SE11 against the inventory ids, independently of any owner control catalogue; their evidence is committed to `REPO-EXT` | SE11-AC-08a |
| SE11-REQ-20 (revised) | Restart timing for the commissioned combination, given charge ≥ 50 % at restoration: T_restart ≤ 720 s; T_service_clean ≤ 1,200 s; T_service_forced ≤ 1,800 s (database crash recovery measured, SE12-bounded); M1 outputs drop 60–120 s after the last device on that UPS is off; N1 is sent only at S6 entry and only where admissible; S-2 HOST-ONLY-HOLD and the S-3 guard outcomes are logged exposures | SE11-AC-15 |
| SE11-REQ-21 | Bootstrap boundary: the authoritative repository, host build, inventory and commissioning evidence live in `REPO-EXT`, configured before any local hardware is commissioned; local clones and the T2 mirror are D-REB; the mirror is refreshed ≤ 1 h once `PL-T2` holds and a repository bundle is written to T3-SUB at every stage gate; the protection states govern local real D-IRR data only | SE11-AC-07d steps 0 and 8; SE11-AC-10 |
| SE11-REQ-22 (revised) | Consumer bootstrap: no consumer treats `admit(c)` as ALLOW, or any level as known, until it has received a `/levels` snapshot, a `HEARTBEAT` or a complete INIT set for that level from the **current publisher incarnation**; on `incarnation_changed`, `history_expired` or `cursor_ahead` the consumer adopts the returned snapshot; a STALE or `UNKNOWN` level is evaluated as its fail-safe value (protection: `admit` DENY; the lease is governed by §6.3b, not by STALE) | SE11-AC-09 (b)(d)(f)(g)(h) |
| SE11-REQ-23 (revised) | Configuration validator: the version-controlled host build rejects any fingerprint whose signalling × restart combination is not in the §8.3a matrix or whose S-3/N1 has `ups_n_circuit ≠ A` or no FC-09c row; any producer registration without a class or without a passed SE11-AC-07e record id for the store build; any `auth = local-channel` registration while `bootstrap_window = false`; any `bootstrap_window = true` while token ids exist in the inventory or KEYS has kind COPY; any network endpoint configuration while the window is open; any part number without a confirmed G1 row | SE11-AC-03, SE11-AC-15 (g)(l), SE11-AC-07d step 9 |
| SE11-REQ-24 (new) | **Lease enforcement by stores** (§6.3b L1–L8): every store holding a local D-IRR class persists the latest accepted lease, rejects leases skewed by > U, permits mutation only while its own clock < `until − U`, freezes on clock rollback > U, resumes only on a fresh lease, and proves this behaviour by SE11-AC-07e before registration | SE11-AC-07e, SE11-AC-07c |
| SE11-REQ-25 (new) | **Bootstrap window** (§6.3c): until KEYS is COPY and the initial token set has verified custody, Part B/C exist only on the OS-authenticated local channel; the transition commit enables token-authenticated network endpoints and removes the channel; re-opening requires a rebuilt host with an empty inventory | SE11-AC-07d steps 0–4b and 9 (d)–(g), SE11-AC-08a |
| SE11-REQ-26 (new) | **S-3 circuit condition:** under S-3, N1 is admissible only if the electrician's sign-off identifies UPS-N and UPS-A as fed from the same branch circuit (`ups_n_circuit = A` in the fingerprint) and FC-09c is evidenced; otherwise the NAS restarts only by M1-N, and a layout without either is a recorded FD-RESTART exposure | SE11-AC-06 (sign-off), SE11-AC-03 (validator), SE11-AC-15 (h)(l)(m) |

### 16.4 Risks

| Id | Risk | Likelihood / impact | Mitigation | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SE11-RISK-01 | Single central host is the control-plane and data failure domain (FD-HOST, FD-NVME-M2_4) | Medium / High | SE11-REQ-05, T2/T3, hosted fallback (ADR-01), REV-HA | SE11, EL |
| SE11-RISK-02 | Non-ECC host memory → silent corruption in the Data VM | Low / High | SE11-AC-04 memory pass; database checksums and backup verification (SE12); ECC class substitute (ADR-03) | SE11, SE12 |
| SE11-RISK-03 | PCI_E3/M2_3 sharing reduces bandwidth or is mutually exclusive | Medium / Medium | SE11-AC-02; Fallbacks A/B/C, Mapping-V (§6.1a) | SE11 |
| SE11-RISK-04 | No out-of-band management; a hung host needs physical presence | High / Medium | Physical console; SE20 auto-restart policy; REV-HA if SLOs require | SE11, SE20 |
| SE11-RISK-05 | Support or availability of a SEL-0 member has lapsed, or no coherent currently-supported configuration of the §11.4 classes exists today (SEL-0 is a candidate list; G1 not performed; QC-AR-03 UNMET, gate B BLOCKED) | **High** / High | Part numbers deferred (SE11-DEC-14); §11.4 classes with open FC-01 … FC-09c; G1 before any purchase; SE11-REQ-12 | SE11 |
| SE11-RISK-06 | Long RAID 6 rebuild at 20 TB exposes the array to a third failure | Low / High | RAID 6; T3; rebuild timing measured (AC-07a); hot-spare option | SE12, SE11 |
| SE11-RISK-07 | UPS runtime at measured load insufficient for the per-candidate budget (notably S-2's 450 s) | Medium / High | Fail-closed §8.4 remedies incl. candidate change; TRG-UPS-BATT | SE11, SE20 |
| SE11-RISK-08 | Seed status: Rev 1.1 "proposed" while v1.0 "remains authoritative" (§0, C6); hardware baseline could change on approval | Low / Medium | Sign-off bound to sha and revision; change control per §151/§104 re-enters this document | SE11, PL |
| SE11-RISK-09 | Every required source and dependency is absent (register, PM09, NT16, EL, SP16); the register is **unconsumed** (gate A BLOCKED); reconciliation may change dispositions, NFRs or ADRs materially | **High** / High | All entries PROV; baseline BLOCKED; SE11-AC-11 gate; SE11-REQ-11 propagates status; unmet dependencies tracked as SE11-DEP-nn; §0.3 encoding | SE11, EL, PL |
| SE11-RISK-10 | Capacity candidates must be chosen before a traceable model exists; H.1/H.3 defaults (M-A, N-A) may be undersized for Stage 1 or the later expansion may fail stability re-validation | Medium / Medium | §5.3 Steps 1–4, TRG-MEM-EXPAND, TRG-NAS-CAP; SE11-DEC-03a/06a record basis | SE11, SE01, SE12 |
| SE11-RISK-11 | No supported UPS signalling combination can be evidenced (S-1/S-2) | Medium / High | S-3 automatic fallback (two UPS, native USB each); no reliance before AC-06/14/15 | SE11 |
| SE11-RISK-12 | No admissible restart combination (§8.3a) can be evidenced for the actual circuit layout: after an outage in which mains returns before UPS exhaustion the platform (or the NAS) stays down until attended | Medium / High | G1 evidence hunt in matrix order; same-circuit layout for UPS-N preferred when S-3 is chosen; M4 recorded as FD-RESTART/FD-MGMT exposure; REV-POWER if an SLO requires unattended restart | SE11, SE20 |
| SE11-RISK-13 | Guest-side `io.max` limiter (QoS-unavailable rows of §6.1a) can be exceeded by a misconfigured Build guest or uncontrolled root I/O; FREEZE under lease expiry halts D-IRR mutation and therefore platform operation | Low / Medium; Low / High | Summed-traffic verification at the hypervisor (SE11-DEC-16); SE11-AC-12b case (i); T3-SUB verified substitute prepared during DEGRADED so that expiry rarely freezes; SE12 policy (SE11-DEP-04) | SE11, SE12, SE20 |
| SE11-RISK-14 | The M3 wake-poll loop repeatedly boots the host on battery after S4, accelerating UPS exhaustion and adding battery wear; a boot interrupted by exhaustion is an abrupt host-only loss | Medium / Low | Accepted by design (SE11-ADR-11): after S3 no workload or unflushed data exists; exhaustion leads to the robust AC-cycle path; host-only boots run integrity checks on next start (SE11-AC-13 covers); M1 preferred wherever evidenced | SE11, SE20 |
| SE11-RISK-15 | The external repository service hosting `REPO-EXT` is a single external provider for the authoritative source, inventory and commissioning evidence (FD-EXT-REPO); its selection and SLA are NT16's and absent | Low / High | T2 mirror ≤ 1 h; stage-gate bundle on T3-SUB; promotion to `LREPO` (§6.3); NT16 assessment (SE11-DEP-09) | SE11, NT16 |
| SE11-RISK-16 (new) | Store or host clock faults: a store clock running ahead freezes early (availability), one running behind within U extends mutation by at most U (bounded); a store without time synchronisation rejects every lease and freezes | Low / Medium | Common time source on `seg.mgmt`; L2/L5 skew and rollback rules; SE11-AC-07e (c)(e); telemetry alert on lease rejection | SE11, SE20 |
| SE11-RISK-17 (new) | Under S-2/M1 the host's only restart status source is the NAS UPS service; if it does not return after an outage the host stays in HOST-ONLY-HOLD (no service) until the NAS is repaired or an operator intervenes; a second mains loss during HOLD is an abrupt host-only loss at exhaustion | Medium / Medium | FC-09b autostart evidence and drill (k) as S-2 admissibility conditions; HOLD alert ≤ 60 s; no VMs run during HOLD (no unflushed platform data); §8.4 remedy 3 (S-3) removes the dependency; REV-POWER if an SLO forbids the hold | SE11, SE20 |

### 16.6 Conflicts

No upstream product, EL, NT16 or SP16 document, and no register, was available to check (§0.1); no consistency with them is asserted, and SE11-AC-11 executes the §0.6 checks when they exist. Against the only available authoritative input, SEED-01, the traceability of §0.5 found **six deviations**, each stated with both sides in §0.7 and SE11's disposition for reviewers to resolve: **C1** switch power domain (§165's rack-UPS clause versus its "critical network components" clause; this document says PD-A); **C2** M2_2/M2_4 role labels (§227.6; SE11-DEC-09, Mapping-V); **C3** single PC+NAS UPS with integrated shutdown (§165/§229.1; candidate S-3 adds a NAS UPS with a circuit condition); **C4** switch adopted before the rack (§166/§230 conditionality; SE11-ADR-07); **C5** reference quantities versus candidates (not a conflict by §162/§164/§238, recorded for transparency); **C6** Rev 1.1 "proposed" status (§0; ASM-SE11-02). Where this document would otherwise have to choose between a seed statement and an engineering requirement, it has recorded the deviation rather than silently following either side.

### 18.2 Provided contracts — SE11 register of record (status: REGISTERED)

**Where these contracts are registered, stated plainly.** The engineering rules place contracts SE11 owns first in SE11's Interface Contract document and later in EL's register. Neither file exists in this run (§0.1). The SE11 register of record is therefore **this section**: each contract below has a registration entry (`SE11-CREG-nn`) carrying every field EL's register requires, so that SE11-INTERFACE-CONTRACT (when created) and EL's register (SE11-DEP-02) copy the entry verbatim — transcription, not registration. Consumers cite the contract id and `SE11-CREG-nn`; the definitions are normative and complete as of this version.

| Owner | SE11 | SE11 | SE11 |
| Defining text | this document v6 §18.2 Part A | this document v6 §18.2 Part B | this document v6 §18.2 Part C (derivation by §6.3a/§6.3b) |

| Compatibility rule | Additive fields only within major 1; any change to a disposition, mapping, budget rule, state definition or status ladder is major 2 | Additive metrics/fields/labels within major 1; renaming or removing a signal, changing a state enum, the cursor semantics, the lease derivation or the delivery/failure semantics is major 2; consumers pin the major | Additive event kinds and fields within major 1; changing the derivation rules (§6.3a), the lease rule or the authentication model is major 2 |
| Conformance | SE11-AC-10 | SE11-AC-09 | SE11-AC-07b/d/e/f, SE11-AC-09 (payload check) |

**Part A — `SE11-CONTRACT-hardware-baseline@1` (the hardware-baseline record).** One version-controlled file in `REPO-EXT` (path fixed by the host build and recorded in the inventory), schema version `1`, containing per asset: the §10.1 inventory fields; class disposition, part-number evidence status with G1 row id, quantity status and candidate selected (§1.2, §5.3, §6.7); SEL-0/SEL-1 selection record and fingerprint version; failure-domain ids (§9); pool ids and CPU budgets (§4) with the Table 5.3-R memory lines of the installed candidate and the Req computation; volume mapping (§6.1, Mapping-D/V) and the §6.1a row in force with D2/S2/L2, V_min, B_max, B_floor and the per-device shares; per-class protection parameters A_max/G/U, `primary_target`, the registered producer per class with its SE11-AC-07e record id and the custody arrangement (bootstrap or takeover) (§6.3); `bootstrap_window` state and token ids (§6.3c); signalling candidate, the admissible restart combination, its S5 status source and `ups_n_circuit` (§8.3a/§8.3b-S5) with B_u, R_u, D_off and the measured timing constants; the SE11-DEC-11 signal catalogue version; baseline status (§17 ladder incl. the unmet gates) and declared residuals; evidence ids (§14). Consumers cite this record and the status they rely on; they never restate hardware facts.

**Part B — `SE11-CONTRACT-hardware-signals@1` (metrics, events, level/lease snapshot).**

| Aspect | Specification |
|---|---|
| Transport | (1) **Metrics**: HTTP pull endpoint on `seg.mgmt` (address and port in Part A), text exposition, every metric prefixed `se11_hw_`, labels `inv` (inventory id), `pool`, `class` as applicable; sample interval ≤ 60 s; served by the SE11 host agent on the host OS, independent of any VM. (2) **Events**: `GET /events?inc=<inc>&since=<seq>` — the append-only log on M2_1. (3) **Snapshot**: `GET /levels` — the current value of every level signal, every lease, and the last edge events. During the bootstrap window the same three interfaces are served **only** on the local channel (§6.3c); no broker or bus is designed (BND-03) |
| Identity: incarnation and sequence | **`inc`**: a 128-bit random value generated by the publisher at **every** start (process start, host reboot, host rebuild — any start). **`seq`**: 64-bit unsigned, starts at 1 in each incarnation, **gap-free and strictly increasing within the incarnation**, durably appended (fsync) before an event is served. The event identity is the pair **`(inc, seq)`**; pairs are unique by construction across all incarnations regardless of clock, counter or store loss, same-second restarts or clock rollback, because `inc` never depends on time or persisted state. No wall-clock value enters identity; `ts` is informational. The current `inc` is returned in every response and carried in every event, heartbeat and lease |
| Cursor and handshake | A consumer's cursor is `(inc, seq)`. The publisher **compares `inc`** on every `/events` call: a different or absent `inc` → HTTP **409 `incarnation_changed`** with body = full snapshot (levels, leases, last edges, `inc`, `latest`, `oldest`, `pub_now`); the consumer adopts the snapshot and sets its cursor to `(inc, latest)`. This is the explicit reset/snapshot handshake; events of earlier incarnations are never served by cursor |
| Responses to `since` (same `inc`) | Let `oldest` and `latest` be the retained range of the current incarnation. `oldest ≤ since ≤ latest` → events `(since, latest]` (empty list if none; the consumer keeps its cursor). `since < oldest` (history expired) → HTTP **410 `history_expired`** + snapshot; the consumer adopts it; transitions during the gap are reflected in the snapshot's levels (not replayed) and the last edges are in the snapshot. `since > latest` (possible only with a corrupted consumer store, since `seq` is gap-free) → HTTP **409 `cursor_ahead`** + snapshot; the consumer adopts it. First poll with no cursor → `inc` absent → `incarnation_changed` + snapshot. Every response carries `inc`, `latest`, `oldest`, `pub_now` |
| Event record | `inc`, `seq`, `ts` (UTC, RFC 3339, informational), `signal` (enum: `SIG-ONBATTERY`, `SIG-SHUTDOWN`, `SIG-THERMAL`, `SIG-STORAGE-PRESSURE`, `SIG-PROTECTION-STATE`, `SIG-PL-STATE`, `SIG-RESTART`), `edge` (`INIT` / `SET` / `CLEAR` / `HEARTBEAT`), `source` (inventory id), `payload` (fixed per signal, below). `SIG-SHUTDOWN` and `SIG-RESTART` are edge events; the others are **level** signals |
| Payloads | `SIG-ONBATTERY`: {ups, state S0/S1, charge %, runtime_estimate_s, status_source ∈ direct/nas-ups-service} or `UNKNOWN`. `SIG-SHUTDOWN`: {candidate S-x, t0, budget_s} — semantics: no further scheduling will be honoured; jobs have ≤ 60 s. `SIG-THERMAL`: {inv, sensor, value, limit}. `SIG-STORAGE-PRESSURE`: {inv or volume, used %, tbw %, summed_build_iops, b_max, v_min}. `SIG-PROTECTION-STATE`: {class c, state ∈ MECH-UNVERIFIED/ARMED/PROTECTED/DEGRADED-IN-BUDGET/EXPIRED, kind ∈ ANCHOR/COPY/none, mech_state, mech_ok, t3_last_ok, t3sub_last_ok, rfail, age_s, a_max_s, g_s, store (producer id or null), **admit ∈ ALLOW/DENY, lease {mutate ∈ ALLOW/FREEZE, until (UTC or null), basis, pub_now, inc, seq}**} or `UNKNOWN`. `SIG-PL-STATE`: {state ∈ PL-0/PL-T2/PL-T3-PENDING/PL-T3/PL-DEG/PL-EXP} or `UNKNOWN`. `SIG-RESTART`: {combination S-x/M-x/N-x, status_source, event ∈ ARMED/FIRED/WAKE-ONLINE/WAKE-ONBATTERY/OUTPUT-OFF/AC-APPLIED/N1-SENT/HOLD-ENTERED/HOLD-EXITED/S6-ENTRY/SERVICE-READY, t_restart_s, t_service_s when known}. `HEARTBEAT` payload = the complete snapshot (levels incl. leases, last edges) |
| Initialization | On every publisher start (new `inc`), before `/events` or `/levels` is served, the publisher emits one `INIT` event per level signal (per class for `SIG-PROTECTION-STATE`) with its current value re-derived from persisted evidence by §6.3a/§6.3b; a value that cannot be determined is emitted as `UNKNOWN`. `UNKNOWN` is evaluated by consumers as the fail-safe value (protection: `admit` DENY; **the lease is whatever the store last persisted — a publisher that cannot derive a lease issues none, and the store's persisted lease expires on its own schedule (§6.3b L4)**; on-battery: treat as S1 for scheduling purposes only) |
| Heartbeat | One `HEARTBEAT` event every **H = 60 s** carrying the full snapshot; heartbeats are persisted and sequenced like any event; a heartbeat re-issues each class's lease **with the same `until`** unless new verified evidence changed it (a heartbeat is never a renewal) |
| Consumer polling and staleness | Consumers poll every **P ≤ 60 s**. A consumer marks every level **STALE** only when **no HEARTBEAT (or INIT/SET/CLEAR) has been *received* for 3H = 180 s by its own clock**. In healthy operation the worst-case gap between received heartbeats is H + P = 120 s, leaving a 60 s margin; a false STALE during healthy operation is a conformance failure (SE11-AC-09 (a)). STALE affects `admit` (→ DENY) and scheduler policy; it does **not** affect the lease, which the store enforces by its own clock (§6.3b) |
| Retention | At least **7 days** and at least **100,000 events**, whichever is larger, across incarnations, within the 1 GB allocation on M2_1 (§12.1); never shortened below the contract minimum; `oldest` of the current incarnation is published; events of previous incarnations are retained for diagnostics and served only through `GET /events/archive?inc=<old inc>` (read-only, SE20 use; not a cursor path) |
| Consumer bootstrap (SE11-REQ-22) | A consumer is **bootstrapped** for a level when it has received that level in a `/levels` snapshot, a `HEARTBEAT` or an `INIT` from the **current incarnation**. Until bootstrapped, or while STALE, or while the value is `UNKNOWN`, the consumer evaluates the level as its fail-safe value. Required default while STALE (consumers may be stricter, never laxer): `admit` = DENY for every class; PL state unknown for SE11-AC-10 purposes; scheduler policy on STALE is EL's (§18.3 row CI-1). Host down (FD-HOST) → STALE by construction; the NAS, switch and UPS have no independent publisher |
| Delivery guarantee | A `SET`/`CLEAR` is appended within **60 s** of the condition (SE11-DEC-11); at-least-once from the consumer's cursor within an incarnation; idempotent by `(inc, seq)`. A consumer that missed an edge recovers the level from the next heartbeat within H and the last edges from any snapshot |
| Security | After the bootstrap window: endpoints reachable only from `seg.mgmt`; read-only; carry no workload content (SE11-SECREQ-08, checked in SE11-AC-09); authentication: bearer token `TOK-B-read` (a `KEYS` secret, KB-3b) rotated at commissioning as the baseline control, replaced by SE17's control when mapped (SE11-DEP-05). During the window: local channel, OS ACL, `svc-se11-agent` only (§6.3c) |
| Conformance | SE11-AC-09 cases (a)–(h) |

**Part C — `SE11-CONTRACT-protection-evidence@1` (producer intake into the protection-state publisher; SE11-DEC-20; derivation exclusively by §6.3a and §6.3b).**

| Aspect | Specification |
|---|---|
| Transport | `POST /evidence` on the same `seg.mgmt` endpoint; JSON body; HTTPS; per-producer bearer token (a `KEYS` secret). During the bootstrap window: the local channel with OS-identity authentication, for `se11-bootstrap` only (§6.3c) |
| Producer registration | A producer is registered in the host build (version-controlled, validated by SE11-REQ-23) with: `producer_id`, owning role, the class(es) it enforces, `primary_target` per class, `auth` (`token <id>` or, during the window, `local-channel`), and **`lease_conformance` = the SE11-AC-07e record id for the store build in use**. A registration without a class, without an AC-07e record, or with `local-channel` outside the window is refused by the validator. An evidence record from an unregistered producer id, or for a class the producer does not own, is rejected (HTTP 403) and logged. Registration of a producer for class c is what moves `store(c)` from null to the producer id (a precondition of ARMED, §6.3a P4) |
| Record | `producer_id`, `class` (KEYS/DB/EVID/LREPO/TEST), `event` ∈ {`MECH_FIXTURE_OK`, `MECH_FIXTURE_FAIL`, `DATASET_CREATED`, `COPY_OK`, `COPY_FAIL`, `RESTORE_OK`, `RESTORE_FAIL`, `CUSTODY_READY`, `CUSTODY_TRANSFER`}, `ts` (UTC), `copy_id` (for COPY/RESTORE), `target` ∈ {T3, T3SUB}, `fixture` (true for -init/-rec fixtures; a fixture record never counts as real data and never advances real-data timestamps), `size_bytes`, `checksum`, `restored_on` (host id for RESTORE; must differ from the producing host), `notes` |
| Derivation | **Exactly the transition table and precedence of §6.3a** (P1 guards: scope, fixture, duplicate, out-of-order hold of 10 min, clock, no regression, restored_on; P2 mechanism broken at `rfail` ≥ 2; P3 age; P4 no-real-data states; `admit`), and the lease of §6.3b. Part C defines no derivation of its own; any wording elsewhere that appears to differ is subordinate to §6.3a (§20) |
| Responses | 200 accepted (with the derived class record); 202 held (out-of-order RESTORE awaiting its COPY); 400 malformed; 403 unregistered/unowned; 409 `DATASET_CREATED` while `admit` = DENY (enforcement violation: producer suspended until re-registration with a fresh AC-07e record); 422 rejected by a P1 guard (duplicate, clock, fixture misuse, restored_on = producer host) |
| Absence of evidence | Nothing is inferred from silence except ageing: `age(c)` grows, states move PROTECTED → DEGRADED → EXPIRED exactly as §6.3a states, and leases expire in the stores by §6.3b. This is the deliberate fail-safe that removes any dependency on an owner's availability |
| Responsibility split (defined by SE11 as contract owner; accepted by each producer by registering) | **Producer:** performs the copy/restore/custody action in its own store, restores on a second machine, reports truthfully and promptly (≤ 60 s after completion), enforces `admit` from Part B and the lease rule of §6.3b in its store, keeps its token in `KEYS`, re-runs SE11-AC-07e on every store build change. **SE11 (publisher):** validates and persists records, derives states and leases by §6.3a/§6.3b, publishes levels within 60 s, retains records with the event log, never alters a record. **Acceptance** of the split by an owner is its registration (host-build commit naming its producer, class and AC-07e record); until an owner registers, its class holds no real data (I6) and nothing is pretended |
| Security | Records carry no dataset content (checksums and sizes only); SE11-SECREQ-08 payload check in SE11-AC-09 |
| Conformance | SE11-AC-07b-init/-rec, SE11-AC-07d step 9, SE11-AC-07e, SE11-AC-07f, SE11-AC-09 payload check |

### 18.3 Behaviours of other roles and their effect on SE11's guarantees

No owner has published a contract id in this run (§0.1); none is invented. The table states, for each behaviour, what SE11 provides, what the other role does, and — decisively — **whether any SE11 guarantee depends on it**. Where it does not, the owner's absence changes nothing in this document's correctness; where the owner's part gates something, the gated thing is simply not reachable until the owner registers, with no exemption.

| Id | Behaviour | SE11's side (specified here) | Other role's side | Does an SE11 guarantee depend on it? | Effect while the other side is absent |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CI-1 | Scheduler consumes the hardware signals and sheds work | Part B signals with delivery/failure semantics; default shedding expectation (§4) | EL/SE01 (SE11-DEP-02/03): subscribe with a cursor; shedding policy per signal; STALE policy; thresholds | **No** — the shutdown sequence stops VMs by timeout regardless (§8.3); SE11-NFR-01/03/07 hold without it | Only efficiency and in-flight-work loss are affected |
| CI-2 | Creation admission and bounded mutation per local D-IRR class | Part B levels `admit` and `lease`; Part C intake; transition table §6.3a; lease rule §6.3b; `KEYS` enforced by SE11's bootstrap store; validator SE11-REQ-23 (registration requires an AC-07e record) | SE12 (`DB`, `EVID` stores register with AC-07e records and enforce `admit` + lease), SE17/SE15 (`KEYS` takeover), EL (Definition of Ready), SE20 (checklist) — SE11-REQ-18/24 | **Conditionally, and the condition is enforced:** the bounded-exposure guarantee for class c holds *given* that store(c) enforces the lease, which it must prove (SE11-AC-07e) before it can register; a store that has not proven it cannot register, so its class holds no real data (I6). There is therefore no state in which real data exists and the guarantee is unproven | `DB`/`EVID` stay MECH-UNVERIFIED; the platform reaches `PL-T3` with `KEYS` as the only real class (SE11-AC-07d step 5 branch); no false integration claim |
| CI-3 | Monitoring integration and alerting | Part B endpoint, heartbeat, snapshot; SE11-AC-09 conformance; the host agent's local alert for E4/S1/HOLD/lease rejection | SE20 (SE11-DEP-07): subscribe, dashboards, §12.2 thresholds, drill scheduling | **No** | Nobody is paged; FD-SIGNAL detection ≤ 300 s is met at the agent |
| CI-4 | Security control mapping and policies | §13 column (a) checks, SE11-AC-08a; `seg.*` design; token auth on the endpoints; bootstrap channel confinement; KEYS bootstrap custody | SE17/SE15 (SE11-DEP-05): column (b) control ids, inter-segment policy, long-term custody and takeover (KB-5) | **No** for commissioning (KEYS bootstrapped by SE11); SE11-AC-08b is the owner's later evidence | SE11-AC-08a can pass; KB-5 takeover waits; SE11-SECREQ-nn remain the ids of record |
| CI-5 | Test execution and evidence format | Test specifications with pass criteria (§14) | SE19 (SE11-DEP-06): execute SE11-AC-04/05/12/12b/13 and §9 resilience tests | **No** (SE11 can execute with its own tooling; SE19 is the preferred executor) | Default percentages of §6.1a/§6.4 remain until measured |
| CI-6 | Capacity inputs | §5.3 procedure, Table 5.3-R, trigger definitions | SE01 (SE11-DEP-03): demands, thresholds, B_floor | **No** | Default M-A/N-A under H.1/H.3; hypothetical allocations (ASM-SE11-03) |
| CI-7 | Economics for local-versus-hosted | Gate G-LOCAL wording, Step 2 conditions | SP16 (SE11-DEP-08) | **No** | Local-versus-hosted undecided per pool |
| CI-8 | External-dependency assessment | FD-EXT-AI/FD-INTERNET/FD-EXT-REPO dispositions from the seed; T2 mirror and T3-SUB bundle for the repository provider | NT16 (SE11-DEP-09) | **No** (the mirror/bundle bound provider loss without NT16) | Residuals stated from the seed only; providers unselected |
| CI-9 | Electrical verification and circuit identification | §8.2 load figures, the two-domain requirement, the `ups_n_circuit` fact | Licensed electrician (SE11-DEP-12) | **Yes, for the stable-runtime baseline** (SE11-AC-06 requires the sign-off on file) and for S-3/N1 admissibility | No stable-runtime baseline until on file; S-3/N1 inadmissible until the circuit is identified — by design, not a gap in the document |
| CI-10 | Register and upstream reconciliation | §0.5 traceability; §0.6 mechanical checks | PL (SE11-DEP-01), EL, PM09, NT16, SP16 | **No SE11 guarantee**, but **gate A of the baseline** (§0.2) | Status PROV/BLOCKED (§17) |
| CI-11 | Contract transcription | SE11-CREG-001…003 registered here | SE11-INTERFACE-CONTRACT (SE11-DEP-11) and EL's register (SE11-DEP-02) copy the entries | **No** | Consumers cite the ids and `SE11-CREG-nn` as registered in this document |

## 20. Precedence note

Where a summary figure elsewhere in this document abbreviates a derivation, the derivation governs: restart and service-readiness budgets are those of the §8.3b tables (T_restart ≤ 720 s; T_service_clean ≤ 1,200 s; T_service_forced ≤ 1,800 s; SE11-REQ-20) with eligibility per §8.3b-S5; admissibility is that of the §8.3a matrix; memory figures are those of Table 5.3-R and the §5.3 procedure; storage budgets are those of the §6.1a identity; dataset classes and the bootstrap boundary are those of §6.2; protection semantics are those of the §6.3a transition table and the §6.3b lease rule — Part C (§18.2) and §14 define nothing contrary; the bootstrap authority is that of §6.3c; event identity and handshake are those of §18.2 Part B; the selection record and gate order are those of §11.2–§11.3; the unmet-gate dispositions and their event encoding are those of §0.3.
